ween the employers m relation to Messrs V. M, Salgaocar and Brothers Private 
Limited, Vasco-i (e-Gama and nine others and their workmen, which was received 
by the Central uov'ernment on the 26tn November, 1971.

AWARD— PART II 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour, Employment and Rehabilita- 
tion (Department of Labour and Employment) by order No. 20(II)/68-LR-IH 
dated 27-7-1968 have referred to this Iribunal for adjudication an Industrial dis- 
pute existing between the employers specified in the following schedule 1 hereto 
annexed and their workmen in respect of the matter specified in schedule II also 
hereto annexed: 

Schedule I 


1 . M/s. V. M. Salgaocar & Bros- Pvt, Ltd, Vasco-da-gama. 

2. M/b V. S, Dempo & Co, Pvt, Ltd., Panjlm. 

3. M/s J. H. Carvalho, Vasco-da-Gama. 

4. M/s. Joao Vincent Fernandes, Vasco-da-Gama. 

5. M/s Nar> idra N. Kamat, Vasco-da-Gama. 

6 - M/s. General Superintendence Co. (India) Ltd-. Vasco-da-Gama. 

7. M/s Superintendence Co. of India Lid., Vasco-dn-Gama. 

8 . M/s Itlab (Goa) Pvt. Ltd., Margao-goa. 

9. M/s Therepeutical Chemical Research Corpn., Margau. 

10. M/s Essen & Co. Behind Cloth Bazar, Margao. 

Schedule II 


“Whether the demand 01 the sampling workers lor a weekly off with wages is 
justified? If not, to what relief are they entitled?" 

2. During the portugue regime all the stevedoring and sampling work at the 
Mormugoa Port was being performed by Labour supplied by the Labour contrac- 
tors. This contract Labour system in respect of gang workers and 
winehmen had disappeared after the formation of the Dock Labour Board which 
regulated the service conditions ana wage scales of f tie two categories cf workers. 
However, the sampling workers were not organized and the Government- approved 


Sac. 3 (ii)] THE GAZBTTfi OF XNDTA: JANUARY 4, 1972/PAUSA 11. 1893 j 


inspection agencies used to engage the sampling workers through the labour con- 
tractors. me j.aoour contractors pmu tne workers very row wages ana mey were 
being oppressed and. exploited by tnem. fahri D. A. Leshpancle, President of the 
Goa Dock LdDour Union, organized tnia labour and me sampling workers joined, ihe 
Goa jDoek. JLabour Union. Phis union served the employer.* ot the sampling work- 
er.-, with a chaiter or demands regarding the revision ot scales of pay, dearness al- 
lowances weekly oils, etc., and subsequently had lo resort to a strike. Shn Desh 
pande had aibo raised a dispute with the Labour Commissioner and the strike con- 
tinued. Some of the employers issued etmige-sheets against the striking employees 
lor an illegal strike and terminated their services. The matter was relerred to the 
Conciliation Orticer but there was no settlement anu alter the tailure ieport this 
dispute only in respect of the weekly ofl was referred to thi® Tribunal for adjudi- 
cation. 

3. The Goa Dock Labotir Union by its statement of claim has contended that 
the export of minerals and ores is being regulated in accordance with the provisions 
contained in the notification published in Part II, sub-section (il) of section 3 of 
the Gazette ol India Extraordinary dated the 20th December 196b and the sampl- 
ing ol ores has got to be done by the inspection agencies approved by the Govern- 
ment ol India and it has to be done at the pon ox loading and no where else. 
There were about fifteen and sixteen mine owners and exporters and there w^ro 
in all about 270 samplers with them. These workers have put In continuous 
service which ranged from 2 to 17 yeais without any bleak but still the employers 
considered them as temporary workers. It is alleged that the various Acts about 
labour such as payment ol Wages Act, .Minimum Wages Act, Workmen's Compen- 
sation Act were simply inooperative in th e case of the sampling workers and 
statutory weliare activities were not undertaken by the management and the 
wages were also far below subsistence level. The union had made various 
demands but Government has only referred the demand about weekly ofl as the 
employees were not getting weekly off even alter continuous work and they are 
entitled to get this benefit which 1 b a condition of service in respect of all sections 
of labour anc’ the demand was justified. The union has in its statement of claim 
given details about th e number of workers wages etc., in respect of each employer- 

4. Out of the 10 employers mentioned in schedule 1 Nos. 5 and 6 have settled 
Ihe dispute between themselves and their workmen who were represented by the 
Goa Dock Labour Union presided over by Shri Deshpande. That settlement 
though m favour of the workers was opposed by the Mormugao Waterfront 
Workers’ Union on the contention that there was no employer and employee rela- 
tionship but I have accepted the settlement and have passed award Part 1 dated 
8th April 1969 and I shall in berief state the contentions raised by the other 
employers. 

5. Two employers M/s V. M. Salgaocar & Bros., Pvt. Ltd., and M/s V. S. 
Dempo & Co. Pvt. Ltd., have by their written statement opposed the reference 
on the technical contention that the appropriate Government for the purpose of 
the reference was the Administrator of Goa, Deman and Diu and the present 
dispute referred by the Central Government was incompetent and bad in law 
It was further contended that there was no relationship of master and servant 
between the workmen and the company and this Court had no jurisdiction. 
Regarding the merits of the demand they have not stated anything but have 
merely contended that the sampling workers were rank casuals and they were 
working under the direct control and supervision ol the sampling agencies 
and they were not entitled to any weekly off. M/s V. S. Dempo & Co. Pvt. Ltd., 
have further contended that at the time of the reference there was no sampling work 
done within the limits of the Mormugoa Port and it was being done at the load- 
ing points and the Central Government was not the appropriate Government. 
It was further contended that the President of the Goa Dock Labour Union INTTJC 
had sent a charter of demands on behalf of the sampling workers for weekly off 
and the workers had also resorted to a strike with effect from 27-2-1968. The 
strike hau taken place without any notice to the management and as the strike 
was illegal the management had decided to serve charge-sheets on the workers 
and after enquiry remove their names from the muster rolls and the workers 
were not entitled to any relief. 

6. Employer No. 3 M/s J. H. Carvalho has admitted that he was working as a 
sampling contractor for the laboratories in Goa and for carrying out this work he 
was employing sampling boys depending upon the number of steamers on hand 
The nature ol the work was of a fluctuating character. He could not employ any 
permanent hands In his small income except 3 to 4 mukadams who were supervis- 
ing the work of sampling boys. Within his small means It was impossible for him 
to increase the wages and give them weekly paid holidays unless the rates of 
sampling were increased proportionately. He has further added that since 26-3-1968 
he was not doing the work. During the strike his crushing shed cum-offlee was set 
on fire. The entire premises were gutted completely. He had lost everything 
including the records pertaining to the business and since the date of the strike 
l.e. 27-3-1968 he had not done any sampling work and the workers were engaged 
by the agencies directly and as the rates were very low h P could not meet the de- 
mand and the workers were not entitled to anything. 

'< Employer No. 4 Joa Vicenls Fernandes, has by his written statement dated 
19-9- 1968 contended that he was a small labour contractor. In 1965 after the quality 



£ IKE GAZETTE Of INDIA: JANUARY i, 19V2/FAUSA 11. 1893 [Pabt il~ 


control come Into force the rates for sampling and crushing work were reduced 
to almost one-tnixd, tne worn also depenued upon the wmms ox the exporters 
and the laboratories. Sometimes he did not get even one steamer tor a whole 
month. Hisi income was insuiUcaient and worxer.s were not entitled to claim 
anything lrom him and the demand is not justified. 

8, Employers Nos. 7, to 10 are the inspection agencies carrying on the business 
ot assayeis and sampling work. They have ojiposed the reference on the ground 
uiat there was no employer-employee relationsmp between them and the womers 
and tneir name should he struck orf irom the rderence. 

0. The Government had sent the copy of the order of reference only to the 
Goa Dock LaDour Union as presumably that union represenied the workmen ot 
these employers. Subsequently Shn Gerald Pereira, President ol the Mormugoa 
Water-front Workers' Union, bad put m ms appearance and as stated earlier bad 
made an application opposing the settlement oi the dispute between employers 
Nos. 5 and 0 and their workmen. Though the Waterfront WorKeis’ Union was 
not made a party Shri Gerald Pereira was heard and accepting the settlement I 
passed an award Part 1, 

10. After this award unfortunately Shri D. A. Deshpande died and though the 
reference was adjourned irora time to time nobody on banal! of tne Goa Dock 
Labour Union of San D. A. Deshpande attended the proceedings. On one occasion 
one Shri Shamba Shivram Bhonsle appeared on behalf of the Goa Dock Labour 
Union and hied an affidavit vvinefi was not also sworn. An Advocate Shri V. N. 
Lawande also put in an appearance on behalf of the union but subsequently he did 
not remain present. 

11. At the last hearing one Shri B., V. Gondolkar who claimed to be the 
Assistant Secretary jjf the Goa Dock Labour union gave an application for adjourn- 
ment contending that the General Secretary of tne union was out of Goa. Shri 
Gondolkar further submitted that it should be adjourned to the next day when 
he would keep his witnesses present. Accordingly it was adjourned but the next 
day he did not appear nor were uny witnesses present and the hearing ivae closed. 

12. In the reference order there was only one issue regarding the justifiability 
of the demand for weekly off. The employers had resisted the reference mainly 
on the technical contentions such as jurisdiction, relationship of employer-emplo- 
yee etc. They had also opposed the reference only on the grounds of financial 
difficulties and the labour being casual, i have already observed that employers 
Nos. 5 and ti had settled ,he dispute. Though employer No. 2 had opposed the 
reference on various grounds they have also entered into a settlement with the 
union concerned, Although the puities have raised technical contentions they have 
not put forth any arguments and the question is whether this Tribunal can 
adjudicate ufipon the reference and the referance is valid, 

13. Though the employers have contended that there was no sampling work 
being done with the limits of the Mormugoa Port and the sampling work was 
being done at the various loading pointy if is c.ear from th e written statements 
of some of the employers themselves that the sampling work is to done at the port 
when the ship is being loaded. Shri J. H, Carvalho, employer No. 4 has in his 
written statement dated 5th February 1909 stated that he was working as a 
sampling contractor for various laboratories and the work he was undertaking 
involved the picking up of the samples from the steamer under loading to crush 
the lumpy ore and send it to the laboratory for analysing. He has further stated 
that for doing this work he was engaging sampling boys depending upon the 
number of steamers on hand. This supports the contentions of the union in its 
written statement that sampling of ores has got to be done by the inspection 
agency which was approved by the Government of India and it has to be done 
at the port of loading and nowhere else. It is not in dispute that quality control 
In respect of sampling of ores has been introduced by the Government of India with 
effect lrom l-4-19t>5. Employer No. 4 .loa Vicents Fernandes has also stated that 
he was a sampling contractor at Mormugoa Harbour and this clearly shows that 
the sampling wmk has to be done In the port and in relation to an industrial dis- 
pute concerning a major port the Central Government is the appropriate Govern- 
ment. The reference Is to be made only under section 10(1) (d) of the Act. It 
Is not made under the rules framed, 1'he Central Government has not also 
delegated their powers to the Union Territory and there is no question of want 
of competency on the part of the Central Government to make the reference and 
the reference order passed by the Central Government i s legal and the reference 
does not suffer from Incompetency and this Tribunal has jurisdiction. 

14. As regards the other contention about the want of relation; hip between 
the employers and the workmen it is significant to note that the main contestants 
have cautiously used the words that on the date of the lefereme there was no 
relationship of master and servant. Employer No. 1 in his wntten statement 
dated 29-1-1960 paragraph 2(c) h.v sta'eq that from 1-4-1965 to 25-3-1908 the 
company used to supply such of the casual sampling workers as -equired by the 
sampling agencies and they stopped this contract of supplying the labour to the 
sampling agencies from 26-3-1968 and it is because of this circumstance they 
have contended that these persons wen; not their workmen. Similarly employer 
No. 2, in paragraph 4 of written statement dated 10-10-1908 has admitted that for the 


Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1, I972/PAUSA (1, 3893 7 


purpose of doing the preshipment si'.npl ng work the company had engaged 
the services of a reputed firm M/s Itaiao Pvi., Ltd. and m order to assist the firm 
for doing the sampling work the comj—.iy naci ngaged some casual workers from 
time to time. They have further eonien .ed th;n because of the illegal strike they 
had charge-sheeted the workmen and removed their names from the muster. This 
clearly shows that they had engaged the workman and the contention that the 
was no relationship of master and servant cannot accepted. 

15. It is further clear from tne definition of “Workman” under the Act, that this 
term includes any person who has been dismissed or discharged or retrenched in 
connection with or as a consequence ot the dispute. The workers had demanded 
the revision of pay scales, weekly ofl and because of the demand they had gone 
on strike and the management removed them from service contending that the 
strike was illegal and it shall have to be Held dial the removal has in connection 
with the dispute and the employees are wo.wnen. Employers Nos. 3 and 4 admit- 
ted that they used to engage labour. Employer No. 7 and 9 oy letters dated 
28-5-1969 and 24-9-68 have stated that they had no labour employees working under 
them and they could not give any information. They were directed to file a written 
statement as per notice but they have not filed any written statement and it shall 
to be held that there is relationship of master and servant between the parties and 
the employees are workmen and this Tribunal has jurisdiction. 

16. I have already observed that the only issue referred for adjudication is about 
the justifiabilitv of the demand for weekly off with wages. The employers have 
mostly opposed the reference on technical grounds. Considering the conditions of 
service of the other dock workers and workers in other industries, I do not think 
that the demand of the sampling workers for weekly off with wages is unjustified. 
They are required to pick up the lumpy ore and crush it. It is hard work and after 
continuous work of six days the worker is morally entitled for rest. Employers 
Nos. 5 and 6 have already entered into a settlement and agreed to give a weekly 
off with wages. Employer No. 2 has also agreed and it shall have to be held that 
the demand of the workers for weekly off with wages is justified and they are en- 
titled to a weekly off and hence my award accordingly. 

17. The second part of the reference is “If not to what relief are they entitled?”. 
I have given my finding on the first part of the reference in the affirmative and the 
question of the second part does not arise and' the further question is whether the 
workers are entitled to the relief with retrospective effect. 

18. I have already discussed tthe circumstances under which the dispute was 
referred. The Dock Labour Union raised a dispute making various demands. They 
had also given a strike notice and the workers had gone on strike from 23rd March, 
1968. Employer No. 1 has in his affidavit stated that they have stopped the prac- 
tice of supplying sampling workers to the sampling agencies from 26th March, 
1968. Employer No. 3 has staled that since the strike of 27th March, 1968 he is 
thrown out of business and has discontinued to v e a contractor on 8th April, 1968. 
Moreover the shed cum-office was set on fire and the entire premises were gutted 
completely. He has produced photographs showing the position before and after 
the fire It is further clear from the union’s statement of claim that it was having 
direct negotiations with the insnection agencies during the months of March 1 and 
April 1968 and a proposal to pool together all the sampling workers was mooted. 
It has further stated that the inspection agencies were convinced that the demands 
of the sampling workers were iusf and legitimate and accordingly they have been 
negotiating wi'h the workers directly. Employer No. 3 Italab (Goal Pvt. Ltd., have 
given a purshis dated 20th September, 1971 that they have been giving a weekly 
off to the labourers provided they work continuouslv for a period of six days in a 
■week. It is not known as to from what date the system of supolytng labour by the 
labour contractors ceased and the agencies started employing labour directly. 
Under the circumstances in my opinion there is no question of any retrospective 
effect and the employees will be entitled to a weekly off with wages from the 
date this award comes into operation. 

No order as to costs. 


(Sd.l A. T. Zambhe, 
Presiding Officer, 

Central Government Industrial Tribunal, 

Bombay.

Part U— 


Now, therefore, m exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-section

Tribunal in respect of the matters set forth in the Schedule hereto annexed; 

And whereas, the- Central Government is satisfied that the persons applying 
represent the majority of each party; 

Now therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (2) of 
Section 'lO of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1047, the Central Government hereby 
refers the said dispute for adjudication to the Central Government Industrial 
Tribunal, Calcutta, constituted under Section 7A of the Act.

(vi) of clause (n) of section 2 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947),

months from the 29th December, 1971,

8.0. 19. — Whereas the Central Government is of opinion that the minimum 
rates of wages should be fixed under the Minimum Wages Act, 1948 (11 of 1948) in 
respect of employment in Fire Clay mines covered under the Mines Act, 1952 (35 
of 1952). 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by section 27 of the said 
Act, and in supersession of the Notification of the Government of India, in the 
Ministry of Laoour, Employment and Rehabilitation (Department of Labour and 
Employment) No. S.O. 1068, dated the 1st March, 1971, the Central Government 
hereby gives three months notice of its intention to add the said employment in 
Part I of the Schedule to the said Act. 

Any suggestions or objections which may be received from any person in res- 
pect of the said addition before the expiry of three months from the date of 
publication of this notification in the official Gazette, will be considered by the 
Central Government.

Draft Notification 

In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (2) of section 26 of the 
Minimum Wages Act, 1948 (11 of 1948), the Central Government hereby directs 
that for a further period of five years with effect from the 24th April, 1971, the 
provisions of sections 12, 13, 14 and 18 of the said Act shall not apply to railway 
servants who are on time scales of pay aooroved by the Central Government and 
governed by the provisions of Chapter VIA of the Indian Railways Act, 1890, 
and the Indian Railway Establishment Code and who are employed In any sche- 
duled employment on Railways,

PART III 

1 . Medical Facilities 

(a) Hospitals . — The three Central Hospitals one each at Dhanbad, Asansol and 
Manendragarh and twelve Regional Hospitals situated in different Coalfields con- 
tinued to function. The bed-strength of the Central Hospitals at Dhanbad. Asansol 
and Manendragarh is 300, 350 and 50 respectively, 

(b) Dispensaries . — One static allopathic dispensary at Mugma in Jharia Coal- 
field, two mobile medical units one each at Assam and Pench Valley Coalfields 
and 29 ayurvedic dispensaries were run during the period under report. For the 
manufacture of the genuine medicines required for the use at the ayurvedic 
dispensaries, the Ayurvedic Pharmacy set up at Patherdlh In the Jharia Coalfield, 
continued to render service. 



Sec. 3 (ti)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1, 1972/PAUSA II, 1893 *i 


(c) Family welfore, Maternity and Child Welfare Centres. — A Family Welfare 
Centre attached to each of the Regional Hospitals continued to function. Besides, 

8 such centres already established tn the vauous coalfields were also functioning 
as independent units, each under the charge of a qualified Lady Health Visitor 
In addition, 58 Maternity and Child Welfare Centres were being run by the 
Asansol, Jharia and Hazarihagh Mines Boards of Health for which the Coal Mines 
Labour Welfare Organisation continued to pay grant-ln-aid towards their main- 
tenance. For the benefit of the ci lliery workers in the Chanda Coalfleds, the block 
attached to the Government Hospital, Chanda, con inued to function. 

(d) Financial Assistance for improving Dispensary Services.— With a view to 
encouraging the colliery managements for improving the standard of dispensary 
services at the collieries for the benefit of 4 he colliery workers and their depen- 
dents, the scheme for the payment of g'-ant-ln-rid was continued and a sum of 
Rs 12,31,756 was paid during the year. Further, in order to give Incentive to 
colliery managements to provide new dispensaries or to improve the existing dis- 
pensary services for the benefit of the workers employed by them, the Coal Mines 
Labour Welfare Organisation operated a scheme of financial assistance in the 
form of payment of interest free loans equivalent to actual cost of construction 
or improvement of existing building for the dispensaries including purchase of 
equipment subject to the maximum of 16 times of the annual grant-in-aid, 

(e) Anti T. B. Measures : — Besides 262 beds provided by the Coal Mines Labour 
Housing and General Welfare Fund, 61 beds remained reserved in the various 
T.B. Sanatoria for the treatment of colliery workers and their dependents suffering 
from T. B. The scheme of domiciliary T. B. treatment continued to function and 
payment of diet and subslstance allowances to the T. B. patients who were under- 
going treatment under this scheme also continued to be made according to 
schedule. A sum of Ra. 6,62,613 69 was paid during the period. For the reserva- 
tion of 61 beds, a sum of Rs. 1,21,955.36 was paid during the year to .the various 
sanatorium authorities. 

(0 'Vaimlly ' PlanninglServtcis : — Free advice on’family planning continued to be given and 
contraceptive supplied free of cost at the Coal Mines Labour Housing and General Welfare Fund’, 
Hospitals and at the clinics openei for the purpose at some rural anl Urban areas. The scheme 
for the payment grant In aid to the colliery owners for the family planning work continued to 
be in force. 5 Static Family Planning Units and 3 Mobile Family Planning Units remained 
in operation. In addition, the Materinty and Child Welfare Centres numbering 58 run by the 
Jharia, Asansol and Hazaribag Mines Boards of Health in different coalfields for which the coal 
Mines Labour velfiriHOrgi aLatlon Ins been paying grant-ln-aid, also provided family planning 
service for the benefit of the colliery workers and their dependents in those areas. 

(g) Rehabilitation : — A Rehabilitation cum-Physiotherapy Centre attached to each of 
the Central Hospitals at Dhanbad and Asansol, continued to function. The work of construction 
of the Rehabilitation Centre at Chhindwara is in progress. The construction of the Reh abilita- 
tlon-cutn-Convalescent Home at Sidhabari was comleted a if necessary action for providing 
electric connection to the Centre was taken. 


(h) Other Activities : — Other important activities of the Coal Mines Labour Welfare Organ 
sation on the medical mi public health side were giving facilities for the treatment of Cancer, 
Leprosy and Mental Cases, running of blood bank at both the Central Hospitals at Dhanbai and 
Asansol, free supply of ipectacles and dentures and Malaria and Filaria control operations.

(b) Games and Spurts — During the year, for organising various games and sports a sum of 
Rs. 1,34,000 /- was sanctioned. The more important events are summarised below: — 

(i) The lath All India Coalfield Sports — The loth All India Coalfield Sports was held at 

Jcalgore colliery Stadium in Jharia Coalfield on 2oth and 2rst February, I97t. Teams 
from Assam, west Bengal, Jharia, Hazarlbagi Orissa, Andhra Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh 
and Maharashtra coalfields participated in the sports. West Bengal Coalfield retained 
the coalfield Championship and jharia coalfield was the runners up. 

(ii) All India Coalfield Football Tournament —The tournament was held, from nth 
November, 1970 to 15th November, 1970 at Chandametha colliery in Pench Valley 
Coalfield. Tne teams of all Coalfields participated in the tournaments. The West 
Bengal coalfield team won the tournament and the Orissa coalfield was runners up.

(Department of Labour and Employment) 

New Delhi, the 21th November 1971 

8.0. 25. — Whereas it appears to the Central Government that the employer 
and the majority of the employees In relation to the establishment known as 
Messrs Nathanl Slitters & Decoilers Private Limited, Adjoining Vidya Vihar 
Railway Station (West), Bombay-86 have agreed that the provisions of the Emp- 
loyees’ Provident Funds and Family Pension Fund Act, 1952 (19 of 1952), should 
be made applicable to the said establishment; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (4) of 
section 1 of the said Act. the Central Government hereby applies the provisions 
of the said Act to the said establishment. 

This notification shall be deemed to have come into force on the first day of 
December, 1970.

8.0. 49. — Whereas Messrs Associated Instrument Manufacturers (India) Pri- 
vate Limited, B-5, GUIander House, 8, Netaji Subha9 Road, Calcutta (hereinafter 
referred to as the said establishment), has applied for exemption under clause (a) 
of sub-section (1) of section 17 of the Employees’ Provident Funds and Family 
Pension Fund Act, 1952 (19 of 1952); 

And whereas in the opinion of the Central Government, the rules of the provi- 
dent fund of the said establishment with respect to the rates of contribution are 
not less favourable to the employees therein than those specified in section 6 of 
the said Act, and the employees are also in enjoyment of other Provident fund 
benefits which on the whole are not less favourable to the employees than the 
benefits provided under the said Act or under the Employees’ Provident Funds 
Scheme, 1952 (hereinafter referred to as the said Scheme) in relation to the 
employees in any other establishment of a similar character; 

Now, therefore, In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (a) of sub- 
section (1) of section 17 of the said Act, and subject to the conditions specified in 
the Scheduled annexed hereto, the Central Government hereby exempts the said 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of the said Scheme and in 
pursuance of sub-section (39 of the said section 17, the Central Government 
hereby directs that,— 

(a) the employer in relation to the said establishment shall pay within 

fifteen days of the close of the month to the Employees’ Frovtdent 
Fund, inspection charges at the rate of 0 09 per cent (zero point zero 
nine ner cent) of the pay (basic wages, dearness allowance, retaining 
allowance, if any, and cash value of food concession admissible there- 
on) for the time ibeing payable to the employees of the said establish- 
ment who would have become members under the said Scheme but 
for this exemption; 

(b) the said employer shall invest the provident fund contributions in ac- 

cordance with the directions issued bv the Central Government from 
time to time. 

The Schedule 

1 The employer shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner as the Central Government may, from time to time, prescribe. 

2 The omnl over shall furnish to each employee an Annual Statement of Ac- 
count or Pass Book. 

3 All expenses involved in the administration of the Fund including the maw- 
tomne-' nf n "coopts. submission of accounts and returns, transfer of nccumulp 
tions rnvment of inspection charges etc, shall be borne bv the emolove-

S.O. 61. — Whereas Messrs South India Insurance Company Limited, Moti 
Mahal, Janisneaji Tata Ttoad, XXombajy-Zu rnereinaijier reieiretl to as tne said 
establishment ) has applied lor exernpuon unuer ciause (.a) ox sub-section ( 1 ) of 
section 17 of the Employees' Provident .bunds and lamily Pension Fund Act, 1962 
(10 of 1952); 

And whereas in the opinion of the Cential Government, the rules of the 
provident iund of the saiu establishment with respect to the rates of contribution 
are not less iuvourable to tne employees therein cnan tnose speciliea m section 6 
of the said Act, and the employees are also 111 enjoyment or orner provident fund 
benents which on the whoie are not less favourable to the employees than the 
benehts provided under the said Act or under the Employees' Provident .bunds 
Scheme, 1902 (hereinafter leieired to as the saiu Scheme) in relation to the 
employees In any other establishment of a similar character; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (a) of sub-section 
(1) of section 17 of the said Act, and subject to the conditions specilied in the 
Schedule annexed hereto, the Central Government hereby exempts the said 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of tne said Scheme and m 
pursuance of sub-section (3) of the said section 17, the Central Government hereby 
directs that: — 

(a) the employer in relation to the said establishment shall pay within 

fifteen days of the close of the month to tho Employees’ Provident 
Fund, inspection charges at the rate of 0.09 per cent (zero point zero 
nine per cent) of the pay (basic wages, dearness allowance, retaining 
allowance, if any, and cash value of food concession admissible 
thereon) for the time being payable to the employees of the Baid 
establishment who would have become members under the said 
Scheme but for this exemption; 

(b) the said employer shall invest the provident fund contributions in 

accordance with the directions issued by the Central Government 
from time to time. 

The Schedule 

1. The employer shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner as the Central Government may, from time to time, prescribe. 

2. The employer shall furnish to each employee an Annual Statement of 
Account or Pass Book. 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of the Fund Including the 
maintenance of accounts, submission of accounts and returns, transfer of accumu- 
lations, payment of inspection charges etc., shall be borne by the employer. 

4. The employee shall display on the Notice Board of the establishment a copy 
Of the rules of the Fund as approved by the appropriate Government and, as and 
when amended, alongwith a translation of the salient points thereof in the langu- 
age of the majority of the employees. 

6 . Where an employee who is already member of the Employees’ Provident 
Fund (Statutory Fund) or the Provident Fund of another exempted establishment 
is employed in his establishment, the employer shall immediately enrol him as a 
member of the Fund of the establishment, and accept the past accumulations in 
respect of such employee and credit to his account. 

0 . The employer shall enhance the rate of provident fund contribution appro- 
priately if the rate of provident fund contributions for the class of establishments 
in which his establishment falls is enhanced under the Employees’ Provident 
Funds and Family Pension Fund Act, 1962 so that the benefits under the provident 
fund scheme of the establishment shall not become less favourable than the benefit 
provided under the Employees’ Provident Funds and Family Pension Fund Act, 
1062. 

7. The establishment shall submit an audited balance sheet of its provident 
fund every year to the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner within 3 months 
of the close of the year. 

8 . No amendment of the rules of the provident fund shall be made without 
the previous approval of the Central Provident Fund Commissioner. Where any 
amendment is likely to affect adversely the interests of the employees, the Central 
Provident Fund Commissioner shall, before giving his approval, give a reasonable 
opportunity the employees to explain their point of view.

SO. 63. — Whereas It appears to the Central Government that the employer 
and the majority of the employees in relation to the establishment known as 
Messrs Alpana Industries, 1/4A, Khagendra Chatterjee Road, Calcutta-2, have 
agreed that the provisions of the Employees’ Provident Funds and Family Pension 
Fund Act, 1952 (19 of 1952), should be made applicable to the said establishment; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub- section (4) of 
section 1 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby applies the provisions 
of the said Act to the said establishment. 

This notification shall be deemed to have come into force on the first day of

S.O, 64. — Whereas Messrs The Jupiter General Insurance Company Limited, 
State Bank Building, Fort Bank Street, Post Box No, 502, Bombay-1, (hereinafter 
referred to as the said establishment) has applied for exemption under clause (a) 
of sub-section (1) of section 17 of the Employees’ Provident Funds and Family Pen- 
sion Fund Act, 1952 (19 of 1952); 

And whereas in the opinion of the Central Government, the rules of the provi- 
dent fund of the said establishment with respect to the rates of edntribution are not 
less favourable to the employees therein than thoso specified in section 0 of the said 
Act, and the employees are also in enjoyment of other provident fund benefits which 
on the whole are not less favourable to the employees than the benefits provided 
under the said Act or under the Employees’ Provident Funds Scheme, 1952 (herein- 
after referred to as the said Scheme) in relation to the employees In any other 
establishment of a similar character; 

Now, therefore, In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (a) of sub-section 
(1) of section 17 of the said Act, and subject to the conditions specified in the 
Schedule annexed hereto, the Central Government hereby exempts the said estab- 
lishment from the operation of all the provisions of the said Scheme and In pur- 
suance of sub-section (3) of the said section 17, the Central Government hereby 
directs that, — , 

(a) the employer In relation to the said establishment shall pay within fifteen 
days of the close of the month to the Employees' Provident Fund, In- 
spection charges at the rate of 0.09 per cent (zero point zero nine per 
cent) of the pay (haste wages, dearness allowance, retaining allowance, 
If any, and cash value of food concession admissible thereon) for the 
time being payable to the employees of the said establishment who 
would have become members under the said Scheme but for this 
exemption; 



48 .THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1 . 1972/PAUSA 11 . 1893 [Part II— 


(b) the said employer shall Invest the provident fund contributions in ac- 
cordance with the directions issued by the Central Government from 
time to time. 


The Schedule 

1. The employer shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner as the Central Government may, from time to time, prescribe. 

2. The employer shall furnish to each employee an Annual Statement of Ac- 
count or Pass Book. 

3. All expenses involved In the administration of the Fund including the main- 
tenance of accounts, submission of accounts and returns, transfer of accumulations, 
payment of inspection charges etc., shall be borne by the employer. 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the establishment a copy 
of the Rules of the Fund as approved "by the appropriate Government and. as and 
when amended, alongwith a translation of the salient points thereof In the language 
ctf ithe majority of the employees. 

5. Where an employee who Is already member of the Employees’ Provident Fund 
(Statutory Fund) or the Provident Fund of another exempted establishment Is em- 
ployed in his establishment, the employer shall immediately enrol him as a member 
pf the Fund of the establishment, and accept the past accumulations in respect Of 
ftuch employee and credit to his account. 

, fi. The employer shall enhance the rate of provident fund contribution appro- 
priately if the rate of provident fund contributions for the class of establishments 
ih' which his establishment falls is enhanced under the Employees’ provident Funds 
iind Family Pension Fund Act, 1952 so that the benefits under the provident fund 
scheme of the establishment shall not become less favourable than the benefit prp- 
vlded under the Employees’ Provident Funds and Family Pension Fund Act 1932. 

7. The establishment shall submit an audited balance sheet of its pro,vlden£,- fund 
every year to the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner within 3 months of the 
Close of the year. 

8 . No amendment of the Rules of the provident fund shall be made without the 
previous approval of the Central Provident Fund Commissioner. Where any amend- 
ment Is likely to affect adversely the interests of the employees, the Central Provi- 
dent Fund Commissioner shall, before giving his approval, give a reasonable oppor- 
tunity to the employees to explain their point of view.

S.O. 69. — Whereas the Central Government was satisfied that Messrs Bhakra 
Cotton and Genera! Mills (Private) Limited was situated In Sirsa area which was 
a sparse area (that is, an area whose insurable population was less than 500) in 
the district of Hissar in the Stale of Haryana; 

And, whereas by virtue of its location in a sparse area the aforesaid factory 
was granted exemption from the payment of the employe's’ special contribution 
under section 73F of the Employees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948) until 
enforcement of the provisions of Chapter V of the Act in that Area by the Central 
Government in the notification of the Government of India in the late Department 
of Social Security No. S O. 3230, dated the 25th September, 1965; 

And. whereas the Central Government is satisfied that the incurable population 
of the Sirsa area In the District of Hissar in the State of Haryana has now exceeded 
500, and it is no longer a sparse area; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred bv section 73P of the 
(Employees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), the Central Government hereby 
makes the following further amendment in the said notification, namely: — 

In the Schedule to the said notification against Serial No. 6 the entry "Sirsa” 
in column 3 and the corresponding entrv in column 4 shall be omitted.

S.O. 70. — Whereas the Central Government was satisfied that (1) Messrs 
Nand Ram Daulat Ram, (2) Messrs Sirsa Cotton and General Mills, were situated 
in Sirsa area which was a sparse area (that is, an area whose insurable population 
was less than 500) in the district of Hissar in the State of Haryana. 

And, whereas by virtue of their location in a sparse area the aforesaid factories 
were granted exemption from the payment of the employer’s special contribution 
under section 73F of the Employees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), until 
enforcement of the provisions of Chapter V of the Act in that area by the Central 
Government in the notification of the Government of India in the late Ministry 
of Labour and Employment No. S.O, 1367, dated the 6th May, 1963; 

And. whereas the Central Government is satisfied that the insurable population 
of the Sirsa area in the district of Hissar in the State of Haryana has now exceeded 
506, and it is no longer a sparse area;

S.O. 76. — In exercise of the powers conferred by section 88 of the Employees' 
State Insurance Act, 1 j 48 (34 ol 1948J, and in continuation of the notification of 
the Government oi India in Lie Ministry of Labour and Renabiiitation (Department 
of Lauour and Employment) No. S.O. 1538, dated the 27th March, 1971 the Central 
Government hereby exempts rexular employees of the Central Stores and Supply 
Division, Delhi belonging to the National Seeds Corporation Limited, New Delhi, 
from the operation of the said Act except Chapter VA thereof, for a further period 
of one year with effect from the date of expiry of the period specified In the said 
notification. 

2. The above exemption is subject to the following conditions, namely: — 

(1) that the aforesaid factory wherein the employees are employed ahall 

maintain a register showing the name* and designations of the ex- 
empted employees; 

(2) that notwithstanding this exemption, the employees shall continue to 

receive such benefits under the said Act to which they might have 
become entitled to on the basis of contributions paid prior to the date 
from which the exemption granted by this notification operates. 

(3) that the contributions for the exempted period, if already paid, will not 

be refunded.

State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), and In supersession of the notifications of 
the Government of India in the Ministry of Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation 
(Department of Labour and Employment No. S.O, 2969, dated the 15th July, 1969 
Shram Aur Punarwas Mantralaya (Shram Aur Rozgar Vlbhag) No. S.O. 2230, dated 
the 27th May 1971 and Ministry of Labour and Rehabilitation (Department of 
Labour and Employment No. S.O. 3366 dated the 18th August, 1971, the Central 
Government hereby exempts the monthly rated employees of the Fertilizer Cor- 
poration of India Limited, Trombay Division, Bombay from the operation of the 
said Act for the period from the 29th May, 1968 to the 26th July, 1972, 

2. The above exemption is subject to the following conditions, namely:— 

(i) The aforesaid factory shall maintain a register showing the names and 

designations of the employees; and 

(ii) that, notwithstanding this exemption, the employees shall continue to 

receive such benefits under the said Act to which they might have 
qualified on the basis of contributions paid before the dates of ex- 
emption.

S.O. 78.— Whereas the Central Government was satisfied that 

(1) Messrs. B. C. F. Finance and Industries Limited. 

(2) Messrs. Gopi Chand Textile Mills. 

were situated in Sirsa area which was a sparse area (that Is, an area \vhose in- 
aurable population was less than 500) in the district of Hissar in the State of 
Haryana; 

And, whereas by virtue of their location in a sparse area the aforesaid factories 
were granted exemption from the payment of the employer’s special contribution 
under section 73F of the Employees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948) until 
enforcement of the provisions of Chapter V of the Act In that area by the Central 
Government in the notification of the Government of India in the Ministry of 
Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation (Department of Labour and Employment) 
No. S.O. 1390, dated the 15th April, 1988. 

And, whereas the Central Government is satisfied that the Insurable popula- 
tion of the Sirsa area In the district of Hissar in the State of Haryana has now 
exceeded 500, and it Is no longer a sparse area; 

Now, therefore, In exercise of the powers conferred by section 73F of the 
Employees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), the Central Government hereby 
makes the following further amendment in the said notification, namely: — , 

In the Schedule to the said notification against Serial No. 3 the entry "Sirsa” 
in column 3 and the corresponding entries in column 4 shall be omitted.

S.O. 79. — Whereto the Central Government was satisfied that Messrs Vinu 
Foundry Mohmedabad was situated in an area which was a sparse area (that Ib, 
an area whose insurable population was less than 500) in the district of Kaira in 
the State of Gujarat; 

And, whereas by virtue of its location in a sparse area, the aforesaid factory 
was granted exemption from the payment of the employer’s special contribution 
under section 73F of the Employees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), for 
a period of one year from 21st November, 1970 or until enforcement of the pro- 
visions of Chapter V of the Act in that area, by the Central Government vide 
notification of the Government of India in the Ministry of Labour, Employment 
and Rehabilitation (Department of Labour and Employment) No. S.O. 3727 dated 
the 23rd October, 1970; 

And, whereas it has been reported to the Central Government that the said 
factory has been taken over by a new Management and is being run under the 
new name and Style of M/s. Saga Windel Engineers Private Limited, Mohmedabad. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by section 73F of the 
Employees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), the Central Government here- 
by makes the following amendment In the said notification namely : — 

In the Schedule to the said notification against serial No. 9 for the entry, "M/s. 
Vinu Foundry” in column 4, the entry "M/s. Saga Windel Engineers Private 
Limited” shall be substituted.

S.O. 93. — In pursuance ol section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 
of 1047), the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of Shri 
K. Saran. Regional Labour Commissioner (Central), Asansol, in the industrial 
dispute between the employers m relation to the management of Sripur Colliery 
of Messrs Lodna Colliery Company (1920) Limited, Post Office Kaiipahan, District 
Burdwan and their workmen, which was received by the Central Government on 
the 14th December, 1971. 

BEFORE SHRI K. SHARAN, REGIONAL LABOUR COMMISSIONER (C) 

AND 

ARBITRATOR 

Present : 

Shri K. Sharan, Regional Labour Commissioner (C), 

Parties : 

Employers m relation to Sripur Colliery of M/s. Lodna Colliery Co. (1920) 
Ltd., P.O. Kalipuhari, Distt. Burdwan. 

Versus 

Their workmen represented by the Colliery Mazdoor Congiess (HMS), Bengal 
Hotel, P.O. Asansol, Distt. Burdwan. 


Avpearnces : 

For employers .— Shri B. W. Bijapurkar, Dy. Chief Personnel Officer, M/s. 
Lodna Colliery Co. (1920) Ltd. P.O, Kanpahan, Distt. Burdwan. 

For workmen.— (1) Shri Joyanta Podder, Joint Secretary, Colliery Mazdoor 
Congress (HMS), Asansol. 

(2) Shri Ram Bhajan Numa, Treasurer, Sripur Colliery Branch Committee 
of the ColJery Mazdoor Congress, (HMS). 

Ikdusthy: Coai. State. West Bengal. 

No. E.l/ARB/71. Asansol, the 10 th December, 1971. 


AWARD 

The Central Government, having received the arbitration agreement dated 23rd 
September, 1971 between the employer in relation to Sripur Colliery ,'f M/s. Lodna 
Colliery Co. (1920) Ltd., P.O. Kaiipahan, Distt. Buidwan (hereinalter referred to 
as tile management) and mcir workmen lepiesented by the Colliery Mazdoor cong- 
ress (H. M. S) Bengal Hotel, P. O. Asansol, Distt. Burdwan (h?i niaftti referred 
to as me union) in pursuance of sub-section (1) of section 10A of tile inuus nal 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) reiernng the industrial dispute between them, the 
specllic matters an dispute being detailed beiOw, to my arbitration, and the Central 
Government beftg of the opinion that the industrial dispute referred to above 
existed between the management and their workmen, oidered publication of the 
said arbitration agreement in the Gazette of India, Part II, Section 3, sub-section 
(li) under its order bearing No. L/1913/12/71-LR1I, dated 14th October, 1971. 

Specific matters in Dispute ; 

"(i) Whether the action of the management of Sripur Colliery of M/s. Lodna 
Colliery Co. (1920) Ltd., P.O. Kaiipahan, Distt, Burdwan m relusmg 
employment to Shri Ramgarlb Koiry, Loader with elfect from 12tli 
February, 1970 is justified? If not, to what le.iei is the workman 
concerned entitled? 

(u) Whether the action of the management of Sripur Colliery of M/s. Lodna 
Colliery Co. (1920) Ltd. P.O. Kalipahari, Distt. Burdwan in dismissing 
Shri Harbans Kurmi, Underground Loader with effect from 12th June, 
1970 is justified? If not, to what renef is the workman concerned 
entitled?” 

2. The union was requested to submit its written, statement under my letter 
No. E,1/ARB/71, dated 28th October, 1971 endorsing a copy thereof to the manage- 
ment, withm ten days from the date of receipt of my letter referred 
to above. Simultaneously, the management was requested to submit reloinder to 
the written statement ol the union, if any, iecelved by them within seven days 
fiom the date of receipt of the written statement of the union endorsing its copy 
to the union under registered post with A/D under intimation to me. The union 
submitted its written statement on 6tn November, 1971 which was received in this 
office on 9th November, 1971. The management submitted rejoinder on 13th 
November, 1971 which was iecelved in this office on ICth Novemt er, 107'- There- 
after the matter came up fm hearing on 10th December, 1971 in the office of the 
Regional Labour Commissioner (C), Asansol. On that date Shri B. W. Bijapurkar, 
Dy Chief Personnel Officer was present on behalf of the management and Shri 
Joyanta Podder Joint Secretary. Colliery Mazdoor Congress (11MS), Asansol and 
Shri Ram Bha.ian Nunia, Treasurer, Sripur Colliaiy Branch Committee of the 



Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1, 1972/PAUSA 11, 1893 67 


Colliery Mazdoor Congress (HMS) were present on behalf of the workmen. On 
that date the parties filed a joint petition of compromise stating therein that the 
instant industrial dispute has been amicably seLtled between them on the terms 
detailed in the compromise petition dated 10th December, 1971 and that I might 
accept the terms of settlement incorporated in the Joint petition of compromise 
referred to above and pass my award accordingly. The terms of the settlement Incor- 
porated in the joint petition of compromise dated 10th December, 1971 were duly 
verified and brought on record. The terms of the settlement. Incorporated in the 
joint petition of compromise dated 10th December, 1971 appear to me to be fair, 
reasonable and in the interest of the workmen concerned, and as sveh I accept the 
same. Accordingly, I make my arbitration award in terms of the joint petition of 
compromise dated 10th December, 1971. The joint petition of compromise is 
annexed hereto and made part of the award. It Is submitted to the Central Gov- 
ernment under Section 10A(4j of the Industrial Disputes Act. 1947. 

(Sd.) K. Shaitan, 

10-12-1971. 

Regional Labour Commissioner (C), and 

Arbitrator. 


BEFORE SHRI K. SHARAN, REGIONAL LABOUR COMMISSIONER (C> 

AND 

ARBITRATOR 

In the matter of an industrial dispute between the management of Sripur 
Colliery of M/s. Lodna Colliery Co. (1920) Ltd., P.O. Kalipaharl, Dist, 
Burdwan and thbir workmen represented by the Colliery Mazdoor 
Congress (HMS), Asansol over refusal of employment to Shri Ramgarib 
Koiry, Loader with effect from 12th February, 1970 and dismissal of 
Shri Harbans Kurmi, Underground Loader with effect from 12th June, 
1970. 

Parties : 

Employers in relation to Sripur Colliery of M/s. Lodna Colliery Co. (1920) 
Ltd., P.O. Kalipahari, Distt. Burdwan. 

Versus 

Colliery Mazdoor Congress (HMS), Bengal Hotel. Asansol. 

Representing the employers: 

(1) Shri B. W. Bijapurkar, Dy. Chief Personnel Officer, M/s. Lodna Colliery 
Co. (1920) Ltd., P.O. Kalipahari. Distt. Burdwan. 

Representing the Workmen: 

(1) Shri Joyanta Fodder. Joint Secretary, Colliery Mazdoor Congress, (HMS), 

Bengal Hotel, Asansol. 

(2) Shri Ram Bhajan Nunia, Treasurer, Sripur Colliery Branch Committee 

of the Colliery Mazdoor Congress, (HMS). 

The representative of the employer and the union named above submit as 
under : — 

1. The inslant industrial dispute has been mutually discussed between the parties 
and the same has been amicably settled between them on the following terms: — 

(I) It is agreed that Shri Ramgarib Koiri, Underground Loader had volun- 
tarily resigned from his services of M/s. Lodna Colliery Co. (1920) 
Ltd., on 8th February, 1970 and as such the union drops his case. 

(ii) (a) II is agreed that the employer named above shall provide fresh 
employment to Shri Hurbans Kurmi to work as permanent underground 
Leader in Sripur Colliery with effect from 20th December, 1971. 

(ii) (b) The union agrees that Shri Harbans I^bmari is not entitled to anv 
wages or allowances from 4th March, 1970 to 19th December, 1971. 

2. The representative of the employer and the union submit that the Arbitrator 
may be pleased to accept the terms of the settlement and give his award in terms 
of the settlement referred to in para No. 1 above. 

(Sd) B. W. Bi.japuRkar, (Sd.) Joyanta Fodder, 

10-12-71. 10-12-71. 

Dv. Chief Personnel Officer, Joint Secretary, Colliery 

M/s, Lodna Colliery Co. (1920) Ltd. Mazdoor Congress (HMS), Asansol. 

Dated 10-12-71.

Now, therefore, In exercise of the powers conferred by clause d) of sub-section 
(1) of section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act; 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby refers the said dispute for adjudication to the Central Govern- 
ment Industrial Tribunal, No. % Bombay, constituted under section 7A of the said 

Act. 



Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1, 1972/PAUSA 11, 1893 91 


Schedule 

1. (a) Whether the management of Messrs Therapeutic Chemical Research 

Corporation, Margao is justified in not granting to their ore sampling 
workmen the benefits of dearness allowance and interim relief recom- 
mended by the Central Wage Board for Port and Dock. Workers at 
Major Ports and as accepted by the Government of India in Resolution 
No. WB-21 (141/65, dated 27th April, 1965 and No. WB-21(14)/66, dated 
the 19th October, 1968? 

(b) If not, to what relief are the workmen entitled? 

2. (a) Whether the management of Messrs Therapeutic Chemical Research Cor- 

poration Margao If justified in not granting to their ore sampling 
workmen the benefits of final recommendation relating to wage struc- 
ture, dearness allowance, house rent allowance and compensatory 
allowance of the Central Wage Board for Port & Dock Workers at 
Major Ports with effect from the 1st January, 1969 as accepted by the 
Government of India in Resolution No. WB-21(7)/69, dated the 28th 
March, 1970? 

(b) If not, to what relief are the workmen entitled? 

3. (a) Whether the demands of the ore sampling workmen in respect of the 

following items are justified? 

(i) Payment of overtime at double the normal rates of pav for work on 

Sundays, other port holidays and for work on normal days, 

(ii) Grant of compensatory off for work on weekly holidays within fixed 

period; 

(ili) Grant of 30 days’ privilege leave, 10 days’ casual and 7 days’ sick 
leave; 

(b) If so, to what relief are the workmen entitled?

S.O. 123, — Whereas an application has been made under section lS(ib) of the 
Payment of Bonus Act, 1965 by Contractors of Messrs Salem Magnesite Pvt,, Ltd., 



94 the GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1. 1972/P AUSA 11, 1893 [Part IT— 


(employer) in relation to their establishments mentioned in the Schedule below 
for extension of the period for the payment of bonus to their employees for the 
accounting year ending on 31st December, 1970. 

•And whereas being satisfied that there are sufficient reasons to extend the time 
I have, in exercise of the powers conferred on me by the proviso to clause (b) of 
Section 19 of the said Act, read with the notification of the Government of India 
in the Ministry of Labour and Employment No, WB. 20(42)/85 dated the 28th, 
August, 1965, passed order on 25th September, 1971 extending the period for pay- 
ment of the said bonus by the said employer by 1 month 20 days (i,e, up to 20th 
October, 1971), from the last date for payment of bonus under clause (b) of 
Section 19 of the Act. 

Now this is published for information of the employer and all the employees 
of the said establishment.

(Department of Rehabilitation)

New Delhi, the 8th December 1971 

S.O. 184.— In exercise of the powers conferred on the Central Government 
under Section 34(i) of the Displaced persons (Compensation & Rehabilitation), 
Act, 1954, the Central Government hereby directs that any power exercisable iby 
it under Sections 3, 10, 28, 33, & 34(i) of the said Act, shall be exercisable also 
by the State Government of Maharashtra in respect of the lands and properties 
within the State and forming part of the ‘Compensation Pool subject to the 
condition that the powers under Section 18 shall not be exercised in respect of 
any matter affecting the payment of Compensation and Rehabilitation grant 
to any displaced person either by way of transfer of any pool property or other- 
wise and that the State Government shall not under these delegated powers 
take any step’ for the disposal of compensation pool excepting the lands and 
properties of compensation pool within the State of Maharashtra as were not 
disposed of before 1st August, 1971. 


[No. 1(42)/Spl. Cell/CSC/71.] 
W. G. PATHAK, Jt. Secy.

OFFICE. OF THE COMMISSION OF INQUIRY 

New Delhi, the 7th December 1971 

S.O, 154, — Whereas by the Cabinet Secretariat, Department of Personnel Noti- 
fication No. S.O, 3863, dated October 16, 1971, the Central Government hast ap- 
pointed a Commission of Inquiry to inquire into and report within three months 
-on the allegations made against certain former Ministers of the State of Punjab by— 

(i) Shri Trllochan Singh Riyasti in a memorandum dated the 20th July, 
1971, presented to the President of India, and 

(11) Shri Satyapal Dang, in his letters dated the 3th July, 1971 and 31st 
July, 1971. addressed to the Governor of the State of Punjab. 




Sec. 3 (ii)] THE tiAZETTE Of- INDIA: JANUARY 1, 1972/P/OJSA 11, 1893 ii$ 


Now, therefore, this No till cation is issued, oy and under the order of the Com- 
mission inviting all persons acquainted with the subject matter oi the inquiry to 
furnish to Uie Commission aihdavits containing a statement of facts set out in 
several paragraphs relating to such of the marges contained in the aforesaid 
memorandum and letters us to which th e deponent is competent to depose. Such 
affidavits must be strictly In accordance with the provisions of Order XiX oi 
the Code of Civil Procedure, they should he confined to the charges only and 
the statements of facts to be sot forth in t‘c affidavits must be expressed iQ be 
related to particular Items of charges. The affidavits must be properly verified in 

the following manner, namely, “that the statements in paragraph and 

..-of the foregoing affidavit are true to my knowledge’’ and in case the 

statements are derived from lmonnation received, “that the statements in para- 
graphs and are based on information received by me from 

(naming the informant) resident at and believed by me to be 

true.’’ If the information has been received from an official of the Punjab Gov- 
ernment and the deponent does not desire to disclose his name, the verification 

should toe that “the statements contained in paragraphs and 

are derived from official file No (identifying the file)." The deponent 

should also furnish to the Commission along with the affidavit a list of the docu- 
ments, if any, on which he proposes to rely and forward to the Commission the 
originals or true copies of such oi the documents as may be in his possession or 
power and shall state the name and address of the person from whom the re- 
maining documents be obtained The original affidavits must bo filed on or before 
December 20, 1971 at the Office of the Commission at. Punjab House, Lytton Road, 
New Delhi, on any working day between the hours of 10.30 a.m. and 1 00 p.m. 
The deponent should also supply sufficient number of copies for distribution to 
the memorialists and the ex-Minister concerned, inspection may be had of the 
copy of the aforesaid memorandum and letters filed before the President of India 
and Governor of Punjab at the said office of the Commission on any working day 
between the hourr of 10 30 a.m. and 100 p.m Affidavits not verified in the mariner 
indicated above or not filed within the date and time •■•peclfied above will not be 
taken into consideration by ihe Commission.

Secretary’ to th e Commission of Inquiry.

MINISTRY OF COMMUNICATIONS 
(P.&T. Board) 

New Delhi, the 16 th November 1971 

S.O. 164. — In exercise of the powers conferred by section 35 of the Indian 
Post Office Act, 1898 (6 of 1898), the Central Government hereby makes the 
following rules further to amend the Indian Post Office Rules, 1933, namely; — 

1. (1) These rules may be called the Indian Post Office (Twentieth Amend- 
ment) Rules, 1971. 

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their publication in the Official 
Gazette. 

2. In the Indian Post Office Rules, 1933, in rule 102, for the words “one year", 
the words “six months” shall be substituted. 

[No. 14/1/71-CI,] 

P. S. RAGAVACHARI, 

Director (Postal Tech.).

MINISTRY OF FINANCE 
(Defence Division) 

(Department of Expenditure) 

New Delhi, the 6th December 1971 

S.O. 166. — In exercise of the powers conferred by the proviso to article 309 
and clause (5) of article 148 of the Constitution, the President, after consulta- 
tion with the Comptroller and Auditor General in relation to persons serving in 

the Indian Audit and Accounts Department, hereby makes the following regu- 
lations further to amend the Civil Service Regulations, namely:— 

1. These regulations may be called the Civil Service (Sixth Amendment), 
Regulations, 1971. 

2. In the Civil Service Regulations, after Article 181, the following shall be 
inserted, namely: — 

“181— A. If the headquarters of o Government servant while on tour is 
changed to the tour-station itself, or his temporary transfer is 

converted into permanent transfer by the competent authority, his 

joining time may be calculated from the old headquarters to the 
new headquarters. In case he actually performs the journey to his 
old headquarters for winding up his household etc.”. 

[No. 7006/AG/PB ] 

K. P. GUPTA, 
Assistant Financial Adviser.

S.O. 170. — In exercise of the powers conferred by section 6 of the Indian 
Coinage Act, 1906 (3 of 1906), the Central Government hereby determines that coins 
In the denojnination of 10 Rupees and 20 Paise shall be coined at the India Govern- 
ment Mint, Bombay, for Issue under the authority of the Central Government. 
These coins shall be exactly similar in composition, size, weight, description and 
other particulars to the coins, which were issued under the notification of the Govern- 
ment of India in ihe Ministry of Finance (Department of Economic Affairs) No.F. 
l|l|07-Coin.|Pt.I(i) dated the 28th July, 1970 which was published at page 4073, in 
part II-Sectlon-3-Sub-section (ii) of the Gazette of India dated the 19th Septem- 
ber, 1970 except that on the reverse side of these coins the year of issue shall be 
shown as ‘1971’ instead of 1970 1 . These coins shall be included in ‘1971’ Proof Coin 
Set and ‘1971’ Food and Agricultural Organisation uncirculated set but shall not be 
available for regular issue. 

[No. F. 1 / 55 / 70 -Coin.]

(Department of Revenue and Insurance) 

Insurance 

New Delhi, the 15 th November 1971 

S.O. 187. — The following draft of rules further to amend the Insurance Rules, 
1939, which the Central Government proposes to make in exercise of the powers 
conferi'ed by section 64UB and section 114 of the Insurance Act, 1938 (4 of 1938), 
is published, as lequlrcd by sub-section (1) of the said section 114, for the Infor- 
mation of ad persons nkely to be affected thereby, and notice is hereby given that 
the said draft will be taken into consideration on or after 31st December, 1971 

2 Any objections or suggestions which may be received from any person with 
lespect to the draft on or before the date specified above will toe considered by 
the Central Government 


Draft Rule 1 

1. These rules may be called the Insurance (Second Amendment) Rules, 1971 

2 In the Insurance Rules, 1939 (hereinafter referred to as the said rules), rule 
48 shall be omitted 

3 For rule 60 of the said rules, the following rule shall be substituted, namely — ■ 

‘‘60 Term of Office — Any person nominated as a member of the Consultative 
Committee constituted under sub-section (1) of section HOG of the Act 
(hereafter referred to in rules 61 to 66 as the Committee! shall hold 
office for a period not exceeding three years or such further periods 
not exceeding three years at a time as the Central Government may 
specify." 




!J2 the GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1. 1972/PAU3A 11, 1893 [Pa*T II— 


4. In the said rules, In rule 61, for sub-rule (1), the following sub-rule shall be 
substituted, namely; — 

“(1) Any member of the Committee may resign his membership of the Com- 
mittee by notice in writing addressed to the Secretary to the Govern- 
ment of India, Ministry of Finance (Department of Revenue and' 
Insurance) to that effect and such resignation shall take effect from 
the date on which it is accepted by the Cential Government or on the 
expiry of a peiiod of thirty days from the date of it* receipt by the 
Central Government, whichever is earlier.”. 

6. In the said rules, in Schedule II, for regulation 13, the following regulation 
■hall be substituted, namely: — 

”13. Disputes. — If any doubt or dispute arises regarding the interpretation of 
the provisions of these regulations or regarding the validity of any 
election held under these regulations, it shall be reffered to the 
Central Government for decision.". 

[No. F. 51(3)-Ins.I/70.] 
M. L. WADHAWAN, Dy Bocy.

Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OP INDIA: JANUARY 1, 1971/PAUSA 11, 1893 157 


Where an Income-tax Circle, Ward, or District or part thereof stands trans- 
ferred by this Notification from one Range to another Range, appeals arising out 
of assessments made in the Income-tax Circle, Ward or District or part thereof 
and pending immediately before the date of this Notification before the Appellate 
Assistant Commissioner of Income-tax of the Range from which the Income-tax 
Circle, Ward or District or part thereof is transferred shall, from the date this 
Notification shall take effect, be transferred to and dealt with by the Appellate 
Assistant Commissioner of the Range to whom the said Circle, Ward or District 
or part thereof is transferred. 

This Notification shall take effect from 1st November, 1971. 

Explanatory Ndte 

The amendments have become necessary on account of the re- allocation of 
jurisdiction of the Appellate Assistant Commissioners of Income-tax in the char- 
ges of Andhra Pradesh. 

(This note does not form a part of the Notification but is intended to be merely 
clariflcatory).

S.O, 201. — In exercise of the powers conferred by Section 10 of the Customs 
Act, 1962 (52 of 1962), the Collector of Customs and Central Excise, Ahmodabad, 
hereby declares OKHA BEYT (SANKHODWAR BEYT) situated on the southern 
coast and NAVINAL POINT (a place In the port limit of Mundra) on the Northern 
coast of the Gulf of Kutch, as Boarding Stations and orders that all sailing vessels 
entering or leaving the 'Gulf of Kutch and passing through the port limits of the 
ports OKHA BEYT (SANKHODWAR BEYT) of MUND|RA shall call at either of the 
above referred boarding stations for the purpose of boarding of, or disembarka- 
tion from vessels by officers of Customs.

i.e. from the mid-night of 31st March, 1971/lst April, 1971.

New Delhi, the 3rd December 1971 

S.O. 210. — Whereas the Textiles Committee has established standard specifi- 
cations for the Purposes of export for the material as defined in clause (d) of' 
regulation 2 of the Polyester-Cotton and Polyester-Viscose Blended Fabrics Ins- 
pection Regulations, 1971. 

And, whereas, on the recommendation made to it in this behalf by the Textiles 
Committee, the Central Government Is of opinion that the mater HI which does not 
confirm to the standard specifications established by the said Committee should 
not be exported; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of 
section 17 of the Textiles Committee Act, 1963 (41 of 1903). the Central Govern- 
ment hereby prohibits the export, from India to anv foreign country, of the mate- 
rial defined in clause (d) of regulation 2 of the Polyester-Cotton and Polyester- 
Viscose Blended Fabrics Inspection Regulations. 1971, unless such material is 
covered by a cr ' ^cate issued by the officer authorised by the Textiles Commit- 
tee in this behalf under regulation 11 of the said regulations. 

Provided that th P above prohibition shall not apply to the export of low stand- 
ard m- ferial which does not conform to the minimum standard required for the 
Issue of a certificate under regulation 11 of the said regulations, if an order is 
received from abroad for the supply of such low standard material and If, the 



1 68 1 HE GAZETfl OF INDIA: JANUARY I, I 


"/I'ACiA : . 1893 [Part U- 


oiflcer authorised by the Textiles Committee in this behalf is satisfied about the 
bonafldes of such order, and the export of such material is authorised by him. 

2. This Notification shall come into force on the 15th February, 1972, 

[F. No. 18/46/69-Tex-A ] 

H- K. BANSAL, Dy. Secy.

(Office of the Jt. Chief Controller of Imports and Exports) 

(Central Licensing Area) 

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 15 th February 1971 

S.O. 213, — M/s, Prcm Export and Import Corporation, F-2/22A, Model Town, 
Delhi -0 were granted an Established Importers licence No. P/E/ 018369 7 /C/XX/ 
30/D/31-32 dated 15th September 1970 for Rs. 1,000/- for Import of Motor Vehicle 
Parts as per Appendix 26 of the Red Book for A.M.71 period. They have applied 
for the Duplicate Customs purposes and Exchange Control copy of the said licence 
on the ground that the original had been lost or misplaced. It Is. further, stated 
by the Arm that the original Customs purpose copy of the licence is not registered 
with any Custom House and hence has not been utilised. 

In suport of this declaration, the applicant has filed an affidavit duly attested by 
the Notary stating that the original Customs purpose and Exchange Control copy of 
the licence have been lost or misplaced. 

I am satisfied that the original Customs and Exchange Control copy of the said 

licence have been lost and direct that a Duplicate Customs purpose copy and 

Exchange Control copy should be issued to the applicant. The original Customs 

purpose and Exchange Control copy of the licence are cancelled. 

[No. F. MVP/20O/A.M.71/QL/CLA,]

support of this contention, they have filed three affidavits.

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 25th May 1971 

S.O. 219. — M/s. Rajasthan Iron and Steel Works (Automobiles), Brahmapuri, 
Jaipur, were granted an Actual Users Licence No. P/S/1020823, dated 5ih Septem- 
ber, 1970, valid up to 30th September, 1971 for Ra. 1,82,000 for import of “C.K.C.A, 
Sheets below 5 mm in thickness” from Rupee Payment Area countries. They have 
applied for duplicate copy of the Exchange Control Purposes copy of the said 
licence on the ground that the original copy of the said licence has been lost/mis- 
placed. It is further stated by the firm that original licence was not registered 
with any Custom House and was not utilised at all. 

2. In support of this declaration the applicant has filed an affidavit, duly attest- 
ed by Notary Public, Jaipur stating that the original licence has been lost/mis- 
placed. 

3. I am satisfied that the original Exchange Control Purposes copy of Licence 
No. P/S/1020823, dated 5th September, 1970, valid up to 30th September, 1971, 
has been lost/misplaced and direct that a duplicate copy should be issued to the 
applicant. The original Exchange Control Copy of the licence, is cancelled. 

[No. P/ll(M)/Raj/AM.70/AU-Raj&Adhoc/CLA/370.]

(Office of the Dy. Chief Controller of Imports & Exports) 

ORDERS 

Kanpur, the 7th June 1971 

S.O. 217,— The following licences were issued to M/s. Hindustani Udyog, 139, 
Dayanand Path, Meerut (Canttfi: — 

(1) P/S/1085180 dt. 27th February, 1971 for the import of Copper Zinc. 

(2) P/S/1685181 dt. 27th February, 1971 Lead and Nickel. 



Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: IAHUARY i, 1972/PAUSA 11, 1893 173 


Thereafter a show cause No, Enf. 11(130) /1970/KAN/400 dt. the 16th April, 
1971 was issued asking them to show cause within 15 (fifteen) days as to why the 
item Copper should not be deleted, from their said licences for the reason that it 
was included in them in advertantly, in terms of clause 7 of Import (Control) 
Order. 1955, as amended. 

In response to the aforesaid show cause notice no reply has been received front 
M/s, Hindustani Udyog, Meerut Cantt, and the stipulated period of 15 days is over, 

The undersigned has carefully examined the case and has come to the con- 
clusion that the party has no defence to urge and has avoided a reply. 

Having regard to what has been stated in the preceeding paragraph the under- 
signed Is satisfied that the item in question should be deleted or otherwise rendered 
Ineffective. Therefore, the undersigned in exercise of powers vested in him under 
clause 7 of the Import (Control) Order, 1955 hereby deletes the item Copper from 
the licences mentioned above issued in favour of M/s. Hindustani Udyog, 139, 
Dayanand Path, Meerut Cantt. 

[No. ENF.II(130)/ 1970/KAN.]

Kanpur, the 28 th June 1971 

SO 218.— A emergency licence No, P/S/1653079 dt. 6 th February, 1970 for 
the value of Rs. 4,000/- for the import of spare parts for shearing Machine, Lathes, 
Powder Press, Spring machine & Iron Cutters was issued to M/s, Ajonta Industries, 
bhuKlaganj, Unnao in the category of actual users. 

Th tn^ fter a . show cause notice No. Entl(32B)/1069/Kan/U19 doted the 29th 
May, 1971 was issued asking them to show cause within 16 (fifteen) days of the 
receipt of notice as to why the said licence in their favour should not be cancelled 
on the ground that they had no factory In exlstance and the licence was, there- 
fore, obtained by mis-representatlon. 


The above said show cause notice has been returned 
undelivered with the remark “Ajanta Industries not here”. 


by the Postal authorities 




I?4 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1 1972/PAUSA 11, 1893 [Pari ll- 


The undersigned has carefully considered the case and has come to the con- 
clusion that the licence in question will not serve the purpose for which it was 
issued. 

Having regard to what has been stated in the preceding para the undersigned 
is satisfied that the licence In question should be cancelled or otherwise rendered 
ineffective. Therefore, the undersigned, in exerclbe of power vested In him under 
clause 9 sub-clause (cc) of the Imports (Control) Order, 1955 hereby cancelles 
the licence No. P/S/1653079 dated the 6th February, 1970 lor Rs, 4,000/- issued 
m favour of M/s. Ajanta Industries, ShuklaganJ, Unnao.

the ground that their factory had closed down and no manufacturing activities 
were being carried on there. 

3. The said show cause notice has been returned by the postal authorities with 
the remarks that the receipient had gone out an unknown address & It was known 
when he will return, 

4. The undersigned has carefully considered the case and haB come to the con-

avoided a reply and have no defence to urge. That their factory.

ORDERS 

Bangalore, the 2 2nd July 1971 

Subject. — Cancellation &f Customs Purposes and Exchange Control Purposes 
copies of licence No. P/S/ 1672024/C/XX/37/X/31-32 dated 30 th October, 1970 
for Rs. 4,035. 

S.O, 221. — M/s. Jain Industries, 19-C.2, Koppagal Road, Gandhinagar, Bel- 
lary-1, were granted import licence No. P/S/1672024/C/XX/37/X/31-32 dated 
30th October, 1970 for Rs. 4,035 for import of C-A- Film-Scrap. They have now 



Seo. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY I, 1972/P YUSA 11, 1893 177 


applied for duplicate copy of Customs Purposes and Exchange Control Purposes 
copy of the above licence on the ground 'hat ,he original of the above Customs 
Purposes Copy/Exchange Control Purposes Copy of licence have been misplaced 
without having been registered with any Customs Authorities and not utilised at 
all and that the duplicate copy of Customs Purposes /Exchange Control Purposes 
copy of the above licence now required is for the full value cf the licence Rb. 4,023. 

In support of the above contention the applicant has filed an affidavit, I am 
satisfied that the original Customs Purposes and Exchange Control Purposes copies 
of the above licence have been misplaced and direct that a duplicate copy of 
Customs Purposes Copy and Exchange Control Purposes copy of the above licerce 
should be issued to the applicant. The Original Customs Purposes and Exchange 
Control Purposes copies of the licence are hereby cancelled.

ORDER 

New Delhi , the 24th July 1&71 

S.O. 238. — M/s. Continental Trading Company of India, New Delhi were 
granted an established importers licence No. P/E/0189245/C/XX/34/D/31-32 
dated 20th July, 1970 for Rs, 1,000. For import of garage tools. They have applied 
for the duplicate customs purpose and exchange control copy of the said licence 
on the ground that th e original has been lost or misplaced- It is, further stated by 
the firm that the original customs purpose copy of the licence was not registered 
with customs house and hence has not been utilised. 

In support of this declaration, the applicant has llled an affidavit duly attested 
stating that the original customs purpose and exchange control copy of the licence 
has been lost or misplaced- 

I am satisfied that the original customs purpose and exchange control copy of 
the said licence has been lost and direct that a duplicate customs purpose and 
exchange control copies should be issued to the applicant. The original customs 
purpose and exchange control copy of the licence is cancelled. 

[No. F. 275/B/IV/2/A.M.71/QL/CLA.]
