Calcutta, the 16th September 1971 

Subject, —Order cancelling both for customs purpose and Exchange control copy of 
licence Nos. PAS/1700570/T/OR/38/C/31-32, P/S/1700571/C/31-32 and 
P/S/ 1700572/R/ML/38/C/31-32 all dated 8th April, 1971. 

S.O. 232. — M/s. Shree Dhanwantari Chikitsalaya, 23-A, Kalakar Street, Calcutta-7 
was granted licence Nos. P/S/1700570/T/OR/37/C/31-32 for Rs. 6000/- P/S/ 
1700571 /C/XX/ 38 /C/3 1-32 for Rs. 12000/- and P/S/ 1700572 /R/ML/ 38/C/ 31-32 for 
Rs. 6000/-. They have applied for duplicate copy both customs purpose and Ex- 
change Control Copy for the said licence on the ground that the original of the 
same have been lost. It is further stated that original licence No. P/S/1700571/C/ 
XX has been utilised to the 'extent of Rs. 11,000/- and the other two licences have 
not been registered with any customs Authorities/Reserve Bank and the full value 
of the licences (t.e. Rs. 6000/-) remain unutilised. 

In support the applicant has produced affidavit to the effect that the original 
custom purpose and Exchange control copy of the licences have been lost. I am 
satisfied that the original custom purpose Exchange control copy of licences 
Nos. mentioned above have been lost and directed that duplicate custom purpose 
an<J Exchange control copy of the said licences should be issued to the applicant. 

The original customs purpose and exchange control copies of the above licences 
stand cancelled.

•Rs. 35,680 for the import of Vulcano Graphite & Silicon Carbide was issued in

favour of General Manager (Stores) , N.E. Railway, Gorakhpur against Prospective 
IDA Credit. 

The licensee has requested for the issue of Duplicate Customs Purposes copy 
of the above said licence on lh e ground that the original Customs Purposes copy 
has been lost or misplaced by them, It has been further reported by them that the 
licence remained unutilised and was not registered with any. Customs house. 

In support of their contention, the applicants have filed an affidavit. The un- 
dersigned isi satisfied that the original Customs Purposes copy of import licence 
No. G/R/2087198 dated 25th Septemebr, 1969 has been lost or misplaced and directs 
that a Duplicate Customs Purposes copy of the said licence should be issued to 
the applicant. In exercise of the powers conferred on the undersigned undor 
Clause 9 of ihc Imports Control Order No. 17/55 dated 7th December, 1955 as 
amended the original Customs Purposes copy i s hereby cancelled.

licence TTo. P/C/2062314/S/AN/38/H/29-30/CG.III dated 25th March, 1971 for 
Rs. 3,89,550/- (Rupees three lakh sixty nine thousand five hundred and fifty only). 
They have applied for the issue of a duplicate Customs Purposes copy of the said 
licence on the ground that the original customs purposes copy has been misplaced. 
Itjs further stated that the original Customs Purposes copy was not registered 
with any Customs House and it was not utilised at all. 



Sec. 3 (ii)J THE GAZETTE OF INDIA; JANUARY I. I972/FAUSA U, 1893 193 


2. In support of this contention, the applicant has tiled an affidavit. I am 
accordingly satisfied that the original Customs Purposes copy of the said licence 
has been lost, Therefore, in oxercise of the powers conferred under Sub-clauso 
9 (cc) of the Imports (Control - ) Order, 1955 dated 7th December, 1955 as amended, 
the said original Customs Purposes copy of the Licence No, P/C/20G23 14/S/AN/ 
38/H/29-30 CG. Ill, dated 25th March, 197] issued to M/s, Dave Bhel Pvt, Ltd., 
New Delhi is hereby cancelled.

rately to the licensee.

MINISTRY OF HOME AFFAIRS 

New Delhi, the 27 th November 1971 

S.O. 243.— In exercise of the powers conferred by section 18 of the Central 
Reserve Police Force Act, 1949 (66 of 1949), the Central Government hereby makes 
the following rules further to amend the Central Reserve Police Force Rules, 1955, 
namely:- - - 

1. (1) These rules may be celled the Central Reserve Police Force (Second 
Amendment) Rules, 1971. 

(2) They shall come into I'm e on the date of their publication in the official 
Gazette, 

2, In the Central Reserve Police Force Rules, 1956, for clause (d) of rule 16, 
the following clause shall be substituted, namely:— 

(d) (1) The Central Government may, having regard to the special circums- 
tances of any case, permit any member of the Force appointed under section 
4 of the Act to resign from the' F rce before the attainment of the age of retire- 
ment or before putting in such number of years of service as may be necessary 
under the rules to be eligible for retirement: 

Provided that while granting such permission, the Central Government may: 

( 1 ) require the member of the Force to refund to the Government such 
amount as would constitute the cost of training given to that 
member of the Force; or 



194 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1, 1972/PAUSA 11, 1893 [Part II— 


(il) make such reduction in the pension or other retiring benefits of 
that member of the Force, if so eligible, as that Government may 
consider to be just and proper in the circumstances. 

(2) The Central Government may accept the resignation under this clause with 
effect from such date as it may consider expedient: 

Provided that it shall not be later than tree months from the date of 
receipt of such resignation. 

(3) The Central Government may refuse to permit an officer to resign: 

(a) if an emergency has been declared in the country cither due to internal 

disturbances or external aggression; or 

(b) if it considers it to toe inexpedient so to do in the interests of the dis- 
cipline of the Force; or 

(c) if the officer haa undertaken to serve for a specific period and such 
period has not expired. 

(4) The provisions of this clause shall apply also in relation to subordinate 
officers and other members of the Force appointed by the Commandant as they 
apply in relation to any member of tho Force appointed under section 4 of the 
Act and the powers vested in the Central Government under items (1), (2) and 
(3) shall be exercised in tho case of a subordinate officer by a Deputy Inspector 
General of Police and in the case of other members of the Force by a Comman- 
dant

New Delhi, the 25 th Nuoember 1971 

S.O. 247. — lu pursuance of clause (a) of sub-section (3) of section 27 of the 
Government of Union Territories Act, 1963 (20 of 1963), the President hereby 
determines that for each of the financial years commencing on and after the first 
day of April, 1972, the expenditure on the following items relating to the office of 
the Administrator of Pondicherry, other than ile Administrator’s emoluments and 
allowances, shall be a sum not exceeding Rs. 2.19 lakhs, namely: — 

(i) Staff and House-hold of the Administrator; 

(II) Motot and other vehicles of Ihe Anunistrator; 

(III) Original works and maintenance of the residence of the Administrator; 
(iv) Secretarial staff of the Administrator; 

Provided that if In any financial year the expenditure exceeds the said sum 
consequent on increase in the emoluments of the staff of the office of the Adminis- 
trator, the increase being oceassloned by accrual of increments or Increase in the 
allowances sanctioned by the Government from time to time, the sum shall be 
deemed to be raised to the extent of such increase.

S.O. 251. — Tn exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) ot section 5, 
read with section 0, of the Delhi Special Police Establishment Act, 1940 ( 26 of 1940) 
and of all other powers enabling it in this behalf) the Central Government, with 
the consent of the Government of the State of Bihar, hereby extends the powers 
and jurisdiction of members of the Delhi Special Police Establishment to the whole 
of the State of Bihar for the investigation of the offences punishable under section 
302 of the Indian Penal Code (45 ol 1860) and any other offences committed in the 
course of tne same transaction in regard to the following cases, namely: — 

(1) P.S. case No. 1, dated 4th August, 1970— Chandil (Singhbhum); 

(2) P.S. case No. 49, dated 31st July, 1970 — Mango (Jamshedpur); 

(3) P.S. case No. 0, dated 17th August, 1970 — Mandar (Ranchi).

MINISTRY OF TOURISM AND CIVIL AVIATION 

New Delhz, the 4th December 1971 

S.O. 256 — In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (3) of section 8 
of the Aircraft Ad, 1934 (22 of 1934), the Centra) Government hereby authorises 
the officers specified in the Schedule annexed hereto to take, for the purpose of 
securing compliance with any order made under sub-section (1) of the said section, 
in additional to any other steps considered necessary, any of the following steps 
also, namely. — 

(a) to inspect and take possession of or detain any aircraft or Part thereof 

or any documents appertaining thereto; 

(b) to use such force as may be necessary for the purpose aforesaid. 



204 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA; JANUARY 1, 1 972/P AUSA 11, 1893 [Part II— 


The Schedule 

1. (a) Director General of Civil Aviation, 

(b) Deputy Director General of Civil Aviation. 

(c) Director, Air Routes and Aeiodromes, 

(d) Director of Regulations and Information, 

(e) Director, Aeronautical Inspection. 

(f) Director, Training and Licensing. 

(g) Deputy Director, Air Routes and Aerodromes. 

(h) Deputy Director. Aeronautical Inspection. 

(I) Any Controller of Aerodromes . 

(i) Any Controller of Aeronautical Inspection, 

(k) Any Aerodrome Officer incharge of an Aerodrome. 

(l) Any Senior Aircraft Inspector. 

(m) Any Aircraft Inspector. 

(n) Any Commissioned Officer of the Army, Navy or Air Force. 

2. In the absence of any of the officers specified in item 1 above, a District 

Magistrate or a Magistrate of the first class having Jurisdiction.

New Delhi, the 19 th November 1971 

S.O. 257. — Whereas in pursuance of clause (d) of sub-section (2) of Section 3 
of the Prevention of Food Adulteration Act, 1954 (37 of 1954), the Central Govern- 
ment has nominated Dr. S. S. L. Varma, Director, Health, Ministry of Railways 
(Railway Board) as a member of the Central Committee of Food Standards repre- 
senting the Ministry of Railways (Railway Board) vice Dr. Banwari Lai, whose term 
has expired. 

Now, therefore, In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of Section 
3 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby makes the following further 
amendments in the notification of the Government of India in the late Ministry of 
Health No. SRO. 1236, dated the 1st June, 1955, namely: — 

In the said notification, against item 7 for the entry the following entry shall be 
substituted, namely: — 

"Shri S. S L. Varma, Director, Health, Ministry of Railways (Railway Board), 
New Delhi.” 

[No, F. 14-1/70-P.H.]

New Delhi, the 6th December 1971 

8.0. 260.— Whereas the Central Government have, in pursuance of the provisions 
of clause (a) of sub-section (I) of section 3 of the Indian Medical Council Act, 1950 
(102 of 1956) nominated, in cosultation with the Government of Orissa, Dr. M M- 
Pradhan, Director of Health and Faimily Planning Services, Orissa, Bhubaneswar, 
to be a member of the Medical Council of India with effect from the 13th October, 
1971 vice Dr, R. C. Bohindar resigned; 

And whereas in pursuance of the provisions of clause (b) of sub-section (1) 
of section 3 of the said Act, Dr- N, K, Chandak, V, M. Medical College, Sholapur, 
has been elected by the Shivaji University to be a member of the said Council with 
effect from the 22nd October, 1971; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of the provisions of sub-section (1) of section 
3 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby makes futher amendments in the 
notification of the Government of India in the late Ministry of Health No, 513/59- 
MI, dated the 9th January, 1960, namely:— 

In the said notificatlon- 

(1) under the heading “Nominated under clausr, (a) of sub-section (1) of sec- 
tion 3’’, for the existing entry against serial No. 1, the following entry 
shall be substituted, namely: — 

“Dr. M.M Pradhan, Director Health and Family Planning Services, Orissa. 

Bhubaneswar”.

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 23rd November 1971 

8.0. 261. — Whereas by the notification of the Government of India in the 
Ministry of Health and Family Planning No 19-39/70-MPT dated the 23rd Novem- 
ber, 1971, the Central Government has directed that the Medical qualification, "MD.” 
granted by the New York Medical College, New York, United States of America 
shall be recognised medical qualification for the purposes of the Indian Medical 
Council Act, 1956 (102 of 1956); 

And whereas Dr Glen Arthur Folmsbee who possesses the said qualification is 
for the time being attached to the Church of South India Hospital, Erode-1, South 
India for the purposes of teaching research and charitable work, 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of clause (c) of the proviso to sub-section (1) of 
section 14 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby specifies — 

(i) a period ending on the 31st December, 1972, 

or 

(ii) the period during which Dr Glen Arthur Folmsbee is attached to the said 
Church of South India Hospital, Erode-1, South India whichever Is 
shorter, as the period to which the medical practice by the aforesaid 
doctor shall be limited. 


[No. F. 19-39/70-MPT ]

MINISTRY OF INDUSTRIAL DEVELOPMENT 
(Department of Industrial Development) 

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 8th November 1971 


S.O. 267/IDRA/71. — In exercise of the powers conferred by Section 8 
of the Industries (Development and Regulation) Act, 1951, (65 of 1951) read 
with Rules 5(1) and 8 of the Development Councils (Procedural) Rules, 1952, 
the Central Government hereby appoints till 9th September 1972, Brig. P. H. 
Alfred, Director (Planning, and Coordination), Ministry of Defence to be a mem- 
ber or the Development Council established by the Order of the Government 
of India in the Ministry of Industrial Development and Internal Trade No. S.O. 
3243-IDRA/6/16 dated the 10th September 1970 for the scheduled industries 
engaged in the manufacture and production of Automobiles, Automobile Ancillary 
Industries, Transport Vehicle Industries, Tractors, Earth Moving Equipment and 
Internal Combustion Engines and Directs that the following amendment shall 
be made in the said Order, namely: — • 


In the said Order: — 

For the name of “COL. S. K. Bhatnagar’’ in entry No. 27 the name of “Brig 
P. H. Alfred. Director (Planning and Coordination), Ministry of 
Defence” shall be substituted.

(Indian Standards Institution) 

New Delhi, the 22nd, November 1971 

S.O. 268. — In pursuance of regulation 4 of the Indian Standards Institution 
(Certification Marks) Regulations, 1955, the Indian Standards Institution hereby 
notifies that amendment No. 1 (November 1971) to IS-2556 (Part III ) -1967 Specifi- 
cation for Vitreous sanitory appliances (vitreous China) Part III specific require- 
ments of squatting pans and traps (first revision) has been issued under the powers 
conferred by sub-regulation (1) of Regulation 3 of the said Regulations. The 
amendment provides the distance between the centre line of the outlet and the 
outer face of the flush inlet for back inlet pans of 5&0 mm. size as 70 mm. This 
amendment shall come into force with effect from 1st November, 1971.

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM AND CHEMICALS 
(Department of Petroleum) 

New Delhi, the 27 th November 1971 

S.O. 280. — Whereas it appears to the Central Government that it is necessary- 
in the public interest that for the transport of petroleum from Well No. NK.L 
to Kadi— I in Guiarat State, Pipelines should be laid by the Oil and 
Natural Gas Commission; 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such Pipelines, it is 
necessary to acquire the Right of User in the land described in the Schedule 
annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (i) of the 
section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares its intention to 
acquire the right of user therein: 

Provided that anv person Interested in the said land may, within 21 days from 
the date of this notification, obiect to the laying of the pipelines under the land 
to the Competent Authority. Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction and 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Baroda — 9, 

And every person making such an obiection shall also state specifically whe- 
ther he wishes to be heard In person or by a legal practitioner.

in the ’public interest that for the transport of petroleum from D, S. No. NKR to 

j£ a( ji 1 in Gujarat State, Pipelines should be laid by the Oil and Natural Gas 

Commission; 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such Pipelines, it is 
necessary to acquire the Right of User in the land described in the schedule 
annexed hereto; 

Now therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (i) of the 
section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares its intention to 
acquire the right of user therein: 

Provided that any person Interested in the said land may, within 21 days from 
the date of this notification, object to the laying of the pipelines under the land 
to the Competent Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction and 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Baroda— 9, 

And every person making such an objection shall also, state specifically whe- 
ther he wishes to be heard in person or by a legal practitioner.

B.O. 283, — Whereas it appears to the Central Government that it is necessary 
in the public interest that for the transport of petroleum from Well No. NKN to 
Kadi — i in Gujarat State, Pipelines should be laid by the Oil and Natural Gas 
Commission; 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such Pipelines, it is 
necessaiy to acquire the Right of User in the land described in the schedule 
annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (i) of the 
section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares its intention to 
acquire the right of user therein: 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, within 21' daysfipm 
the date of this notification, object to the laying of the pipelines under the land 
to the Competent Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction and 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Baroda — 9. 

And every person making such an objection shall also state specifically whe- 
ther he wishes to he heard in person or bv a legal practitioner.

in the public interest that for the transport of petroleum from D. S. NKP 
to Kadi-I in Gujarat State, Pipelines should be laid by the Oil and Natural Gas 
Commission; 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such Pipelines, it is 
necessary to acquire the Right of User in the land described in the schedule 
annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (i) of the 
section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 
1982 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares its intention to 
acquire the right of user therein: 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, within 21 days from 
the dote of this notification, object to the laying of the pipelines under the land 
to the Competent Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction and 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Baroda — 9, 

And every person making such an objection shall also state specifically whe- 
ther he wishes to he heard in person or by a legal practitioner,

8.0. 285. — Whereas it appears to the Central Government that it is neces- 
sary in the public interest that for ground bedmg and wire laying from Bunk 
to ground bed for Cathodic protection to Collector line (near G.G.S- IV) Kalol 
oil Field in Gujarat State, Cathodic protection should be provided by the Oil 
and Natural Gas Commission. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such ground beding and 
wire laying, it is necessary to acquire the Right of User in land described in the 
schedule annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (i) of the 
Section 3 of the Petroleum pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares its intention to 
acquire the right of user therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, within 21 days from 
the date of this notification, object to the laying of the pipelines under the land to 
the Competent Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction and 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Baroda-9. 

And every person making such an objection shall state specifically whether 
he wises to be heard in person or by a legal practitioner.

S.O. 287. — Whereas it appears to the Central Government that it is necessary 
in the public interest that for the transport of petroleum products between the 
Haldia Port (District-Midnapore) and Rajbandh Delivery Point (District-Rurdw*n ) 
of Indian Oil Corporation Limited (Pipelines) in West Bengal State, pipelines 
should be laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited (Pipelines) and that for the 
purpose of laying such pipelines it is necessary to acquire the right of user in the 
land described in the schedule annexed hereto; 

NchV, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by Sub-Section (1) of Sec- 
tion 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 1962 
(50 of 1962) the Central Government heTeby declares its intention to acquire the 
right of user therein. 

Any person interested in the said land may within 21 days from the date of this 
Notification object to the laying of pipelines under the tend, to Competent Authority 
at 9, Syed Amir Ali Avenue (3rd Floor), Calcutta-17, in the Office of the Indian Oil 
Corporation Limited (Pipelines). Every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by legal practitioner.

S.O. 288 . — Whereas it appears to the Central Government that it is necessary in 
the public Interest that for the transport of petroleum products between the Haidia 
Port (District-Midnapore) and Ralbandh Delivery Point (District — Burdwan) of 
Indian Oil Coiporation Limited (Pipelines) In West Bengal SLate, pipelines should 
be laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited (Pipelines) and that for the purpose 
of laying such pipelines it is necessary to acquire the right of user in the land 
described in the schedule annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by Sub-Section ( 1 ) of Sec- 
tion 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of (Right of User in land) Act. 1902 
(50 of 1902 ) the Central Government hereby declares its intention to acquire the 
right of user therein. 

Any person interested In the said land may within 21 days from the date of this 
Notification obieet to the laying of Pipelines under the land, to Competent Authority 
at 9 . Syed Amir Ali Avenue ( 3 rd Floor). Calcutta- 17 , in the oflice of the Indian OH 
Corporation Limited (Pipelines). Every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by legal practitioner.

S.O. 289.— Whereas it appears to the Central Government that it is necessary in 
Ihe public Interest that for the transport of petroleum products between the Haldta 
Port (Oistrict-Midnapore) and Rajbandh Delivery Point (District — Burd wan) of 
Indian Oil Corporation Limited (Pipelines) in West Bengal State, pipelines should 
he laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited (Pipelines) and that for the purpose 
ot laying such pipelines it is necessary to acquire the right of user m the land 
described in the sebedu e annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by Sub-Section (1) of Sec- 
tion 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act. 1962 
(50 of 1962) the Central Government hereby declares its intention to acquire the 
right of user therein. 

Any person interested in the said land may within 21 days from the date of this 
Notification object to the laying of pipelines under the land, to Competent Authority 
at 9. Syed Amir Ali Avenue (3rd Floor), Calcutta-! 7, In the office of the Indian Oil 
Corporation Limited (Pipelines). Every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard In person or by legal practitioner.

MINISTRY OF STEEL AND MINES 
(Department of Mines) 

New Delhi, the 18th December 1971 

S.O. 290. — Whereas by the notification of the Government of India in the late 
Ministry of Petroleum and Chemicals and Mines and Metals (Department of Mines 
and Metals) No. S.O. 1516 dated the 13lh April, 1970, under sub-section (1) of Sec- 
tion 7 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 
1957), the Central Government gave notice of its intention to acquire the lands and 
mining rights in the locality specified in the Schedule appended to that notification; 

And whereas the competent authority in pursuance of section 8 of the said Act 
has made his report to the Central Government; 

And whereas the Central Government, after considering the said report and after 
consulting the Government of Madhya Pradesh, is satisfied that: — 

(a) the lands measuring 81,20 acres (approximately) or 32.80 hectares (ap- 

proximately), described in Schedule ‘A’ appended hereto; and 

(b) the rights to mine, quarry, bore, dig and search for, win, work and carry 

away minerals in the lands measuring 422.00 acres (approximately) or 
170.91 hectares (approximately) described In Schedule 'B’ appended 
hereto, 

should be acquired; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of section 
9 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby declares that the lands measuring 
81.20 acres (approximately) or 32.86 hectares (approximately) described in the said 
Schedule ‘A’ and the rights to mine, quarry, bore, dig and search for, win, work, 
and carry away minerals In the lands measuring 422.00 acres (approximately) or 
170.91 hectares (approximately), described in the said Schedule ‘B’ are hereby 
acquired. 

The Plans of the area covered by this notification may be inspected in the Office 
of the Collector, Betul (Madhya Pradesh), or in the Office of the Coal Controller, 
1 . Council House Street, Calcutta or in the office of the National Coal Development 
Corporation Limited (Revenue Section). Darbhanga House, Ranchi.

ALIGARH MUSLIM UNIVERSITY 
STATEMENT OF ACCOUNTS, 1967-68 AND AUDIT REPORT 
S.O. 291.— 

Audit Report on the Accounts of the Aligarh Muslim. University, Alwarh for the 

Year 1907-68 


1. Polytechnic for Girls — 

On the recommendation o 1 the All-India Council for Technical Education, a 
three-year diploma course was started for girls m October, I960 in a polytechnic 
The expenditure was to be met from out of grants by the University Grants 
Commission. The courses were electronics engineering, architectural assistant- 
ship and costume design dress-making An annual m-take of 20 students in each 
course was contemplated The admissions to the first two courses were aa 
follows: — 


Year 

No. admitted in 
electronics engineering 
first year 

No, admitted in^ 
architectural assistantship 
firBt year 

1966-67 

8 

19 

1967-68 

* 5 

4 

1968-69 

6 

* 


The admission in the two courses was far below the estimated annual m-take. 

In October, 1967, the Principal, University Polytechnic suggested, because 
of the poor intake, abolition of the course of architectural assistantship It was 
abolished in July 1968 and instead, a two-year diploma course in office assistant- 
ship and secretarial practice was started .(August, 1968) and 13 trainees were 
admitted to it. Expenditure for this course is being met from the allotment 
sanctioned for architectural assistantship. In May, 1968, the University applied 
to the University Grants Commission for approval of the change. The approval 
is still awaited (August, 1969). 

2 University Brick Kiln — 

The University owns a brick kiln which is run through a contractor Accord- 
ing to the terms and conditions of the contract entered into for three years (1965- 
66 to 1967-68), for manufacture and supply of bncks the University was to pro- 
vide to the contractor coal @ 22 tonnes per lakh of bricks m the first year and 
@ 20 tonnes per lakh in the subsequent years, provided that if the consumption 
of coal exceeded this limit the cost of such excess upto 2 tonnes of coal con- 
sumed was to be recovered at Rs 64 per tonne and if the consumption went up 
still further the rate of recovery was to be Rs 89 per tonne on the entire excess 
quantity of coal consumed During 1965-66 to 1967-68, the University supplied 
408.10 tonnes of coal in excess of the quantity required to be supplied according 
to the contract The cost at the above rates (Rs 36 321) was still to be recovered 
from the contractor (August, 1969) 

Against the minimum stipulated quantity of 60 lakh bricks, the contractor 
supplied only 30 77 lakh bricks during the period of the contract Because of the 
short supply, the University had to purchase 3 lakh first class bricks at Rs. 65 per 
thousand and 4 7 lakh second class bricks at Rs 61 per thousand from the open 
market during 1967-08, The issue rate of bricks from the kiln to the University 
was Rs, 35 50 per thousand for first class bricks and Rs 33 50 per thousand for 
second class bricks On this basis, the additional expenditure incurred on the 
purchase of bricks from the open market was Rs 21,775 (including carriage 
charges). According to the terms of the contract, the contractor was to pay to 
the University the entire loss and damage caused by non-fulfilment of the condi- 
tions of the contract It has been stated by the University (August, 1969) that the 
question of imposition of penalty for the short supply is under consideration. 

3 Loss of Revenue — 

For the Medical College 79 residences (for professors, readers and lecturers') 
costing Rs 28 83 lakhs and 112 quirters (for ministerial staff) costing Rs 15 83 
lakhs weie got constructed through a contractor and were taken over by the 
University on ‘’8th February, 1968 and 31 July, respectively Owing to non- 
availability of water supply, electric Installations and approach roads, these 
residences and quarters have not so far (August, 1969) been let out. It was 
stated (November, 1968) by the University authorities that the above works could 
not be taken up along with the civil works owing to lack of proper co-ordination 
by the architect (a private person) and non-availability of funds The loss of 
rent (till August, 1989 at the rate of 10 per cent of minimum of the pay scales 
of these for whom the quarters were constructed) is Rs 1 48 lakhs approximately, 

Also Rs 0 28 lakh have been sp-mt (upto July, 1969) on the pay and allow- 
ances of 12 chowkidars for the buildings 



Sec. 3 (ii)J THE GAZETTE OF INDIA; JANUARY 1, ly72,PAUSA II, 1893 367 


4 . Improper Maintenance of Cash Receipt Books — 

According to paragraph 165 ol the Manual o I Office Procedure 0 1 the Finance 
and Accounts Department, 1968 all receipt books are to be got printed by the 
Accounts Officer ol the Central Accounts Office, kept in his personal < ustody and 
treated as classified documents. This regulation has, however, not been strictly 
followed as many departments/colleges of the University (a few mentioned be- 
low) have themselves got printed their own receipt books and brought them into 
use without obtaining the receipt books from the Central Accounts Office: — 

1. Medical College 

2. Tibbiya College 

3. Building Department 

4 . Estate Office 

5. Agriculture Department 

6. Horticulture Department 

7. Proctor’s Office 

8. Gust House 

9. Library 

10. Botany Department 

11. Engineering College Workshop, etc. 

In November, 1968, the Treasurer issued a directive to all the departments, 
colleges/schools for stopping use of their own receipt books and obtaining their 
requirements from the Central Accounts Office only. These instructions have 
not been implemented (August, 1969), 

Also, there was no periodical verification of the total number of receipt books. 
Lack of control over printing, custody and distribution of receipt books and 
absence of periodical verification thereof can facilitate misappropriation of Uni- 
versity receipts,

AUDIT CERTIFICATE 

I have examined the foregoing accounts and the Balance Sheet of the Aligarh 
Muslim University,, ^llgarh, and obtained all the Information and explanations 
that I have required and subject to the observations In the separate Audit Report, 
I certify, as a result of my audit, that in my opinion these accounts and the 
Balance Sheet are properly drawn up so as to exhibit a true and fair view of 
the state of affairs qt the Aligarh Muslim University, Aligarh, according to the 
best of my information and explanations given to me and as shown by the books 
of the Aligarh Muslim University, Aligarh. 

Allahabad: N. K. BHATTACHARYYA, 

The 3rd October, 1969. Accountant General, Uttar Pradesh. 

Countersigned. 

New Delhi: S. RANGANATHAN, 

The 9th October, 1969. Comptroller and Auditor General of India.
